w_{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&w_{2}\end{bmatrix}\right)-E\begin{bmatrix}w_{1}\\
-w_{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}w_{1}&-w_{2}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\succeq 0

since
R(\tau)+E\geq 0
.

Qualitative intersection term bounds

Just as in
4.19
, the next proposition captures the fact that when we compose shapes
\sigma,\gamma,\gamma^{T},\sigma^{\prime T}
, in order for
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}\circ\sigma^{\prime T}}
to be nonzero, the parities of the degrees of the merged vertices should add up correspondingly.
Just as in the tensor PCA application, we similarly define
H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho^{\prime}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}
and
H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}
. The following propositions are simple and proved the same way.

Proposition 4.21
.

For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
, for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
, there exists a set of parity vectors
P_{\gamma}\subseteq{\mathcal{P}}_{U}
such that

H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}=\sum_{\rho\in P_{\gamma}}H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho}^{-\gamma,\gamma}
.

Proposition 4.22
.

For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
, for
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,
H_{\gamma}^{\prime}=\sum_{\rho\in P_{\gamma}}H_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}^{\prime}
.

We will now define vectors which are truncations of
v_{\rho,x_{U_{2}}}
. This definition and the following proposition are mostly a matter of technicality and they are essentially similar to the PSD mass condition analysis.

Definition 4.23
.

Let
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
, and let
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
. Let
x_{i}
for
i\in U_{2}
be variables. Denote them collectively as
x_{U_{2}}
. For
\rho\in{\mathcal{P}}_{U}
, define
v_{\rho,x_{U_{2}}}^{-\gamma}
to be the vector indexed by left shapes
\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}
such that the
\sigma
th entry is
v_{\rho,x_{U_{2}}}(\sigma)
if
|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|\leq D_{V}
and
0
otherwise.

With this, we can decompose each slice
H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho}^{-\gamma,\gamma}
.

Proposition 4.24
.

For any
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
, and for any
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
, suppose we take
\rho\in P_{\gamma}
. When we compose
\gamma
with
\gamma^{T}
to get
\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}
, let
U^{\prime}=(U_{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}})_{2},V^{\prime}=(V_{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}})_{2}
be the type
2
vertices in
U_{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}},V_{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}
respectively. And let
W^{\prime}
be the set of type
2
vertices in
\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}
that were identified in the composition when we set
V_{\gamma}=U_{\gamma}^{T}
. Let
x_{i}
for
i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}
be random variables independently sampled from
{\mathcal{D}}
. Define
x_{U^{\prime}}
(resp.
x_{V^{\prime}},x_{W^{\prime}}
) to be the subset of variables
x_{i}
for
i\in U^{\prime}
(resp.
i\in V^{\prime},i\in W^{\prime}
). Then,

H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho}^{-\gamma,\gamma}=\frac{1}{|Aut(V)|}S(\gamma)^{2}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

Proof.

Fix
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U,\rho}
such that
|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|,|V(\sigma^{\prime}\circ\gamma)|\leq D_{V}
. Note that for
t\in\{1,2\}
,
|\sigma|_{t}-\frac{|V_{\sigma}|_{t}}{2}+|\sigma^{\prime}|_{t}-\frac{|V_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{t}}{2}+2(|\gamma|_{t}-\frac{|U_{\gamma}|_{t}+|V_{\gamma}|_{t}}{2})=|\sigma\circ\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}\circ\sigma^{\prime T}|_{t}
. We can easily verify the equality using
Definition

4.3
and
Definition

4.15
.

Proposition 4.25
.

For any
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
, and for any
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
, suppose we take
\rho\in{\mathcal{P}}_{U}
. Then,

H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}=\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{U_{2}}\sim{\mathcal{D}}^{U_{2}}}\left[(v_{\rho,y_{U_{2}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,y_{U_{2}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

We can finally show the qualitative intersection term bounds.

Proof of the qualitative intersection term bounds
Lemma

4.10
.

Let
U^{\prime},V^{\prime},W^{\prime}
be defined as in
4.24
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{|Aut(V)|}{|Aut(U)|}\cdot\frac{1}{S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in P_{\gamma}}\frac{|Aut(V)|}{|Aut(U)|}\cdot\frac{1}{S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}}H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rho\in P_{\gamma}}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\cdot\frac{1}{R(\gamma)^{2}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

We will now prove that, for all
\rho\in P_{\gamma}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\cdot\frac{1}{R(\gamma)^{2}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]\preceq H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}

which reduces to proving that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{R(\gamma)^{2}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\preceq 2\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{U_{2}}\sim{\mathcal{D}}^{U_{2}}}\left[(v_{\rho,y_{U_{2}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,y_{U_{2}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}+(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

where the last equality followed from linearity of expectation and the fact that
U^{\prime}\equiv V^{\prime}\equiv U_{2}
.

Since
H_{Id_{V},\rho,\rho}^{-\gamma,\gamma}
is symmetric, we have

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

So, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R(\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}+(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\preceq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}+(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right]

We will prove that for every sampling of the
x_{i}
from
{\mathcal{D}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R(\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\left((v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}+(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})\left(\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}\right)(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}\right)

\displaystyle\preceq(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}+(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})(v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma})^{T}

Then, taking expectations will give the result. Indeed, fix a sampling of the
x_{i}
from
{\mathcal{D}}
. Let
E=\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}x_{i}^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}
and let
w_{1}=v_{\rho,x_{U^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma},w_{2}=v_{\rho,x_{V^{\prime}}}^{-\gamma}
. Then, the inequality we need to show is

\frac{E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}w_{2}^{T}+w_{2}w_{1}^{T})\preceq w_{1}w_{1}^{T}+w_{2}w_{2}^{T}

Now, since
|x_{i}|\leq C_{disc}\sqrt{D_{E}}
for all
i
, we have
|E|\leq\prod_{i\in U^{\prime}\cup W^{\prime}\cup V^{\prime}}(C_{disc}\sqrt{D_{E}})^{deg^{\gamma\circ\gamma^{T}}(i)}=R(\gamma)^{2}
.
If
E\geq 0
, using
\frac{E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}-w_{2})(w_{1}-w_{2})^{T}\succeq 0
gives

\displaystyle\frac{E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}w_{2}^{T}+w_{2}w_{1}^{T})

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}w_{1}^{T}+w_{2}w_{2}^{T})\preceq w_{1}w_{1}^{T}+w_{2}w_{2}^{T}

since
0\leq E\leq R(\gamma)^{2}
.
And if
E<0
, using
\frac{-E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}+w_{2})(w_{1}+w_{2})^{T}\succeq 0
gives

\displaystyle\frac{E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}w_{2}^{T}+w_{2}w_{1}^{T})

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{-E}{R(\gamma)^{2}}(w_{1}w_{1}^{T}+w_{2}w_{2}^{T})\preceq w_{1}w_{1}^{T}+w_{2}w_{2}^{T}

since
0\leq-E\leq R(\gamma)^{2}
.
Finally, we use the fact that for all
\rho\in{\mathcal{P}}_{U}
, we have
H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}\succeq 0
which can be proved the same way as the proof of
Lemma

4.5
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{|Aut(V)|}{|Aut(U)|}\cdot\frac{1}{S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle\preceq\sum_{\rho\in P_{\gamma}}H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}\preceq\sum_{\rho\in{\mathcal{P}}_{U}}H^{\prime}_{\gamma,\rho,\rho}=H^{\prime}_{\gamma}

3
Intuition for quantitative bounds

In this section, we will give some intuition on the bounds needed for our main theorem
Theorem

2.3
, which is formally proved in
Section

3
. Informally, the theorem states that when
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}}
and
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
, then
\Lambda\succeq 0
with high probability.

We will try and understand why the inequality
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\tau\circ\sigma^{\prime T}}^{2}\left\lVert M_{\tau}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}\lambda_{\sigma^{\prime}\circ\sigma^{\prime T}}
holds. Assume for simplicity that
d<n
and consider the shapes in
Fig.

4
. The assumption
d<n
is used in this example since otherwise, if
d>n
, the decomposition differs from what’s shown in the figure.

Figure 4
:
Shapes
\sigma\circ\tau_{1}\circ\sigma^{T},\sigma\circ\tau_{2}\circ\sigma^{T}
and
\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}
. All edges have label
1
.

Firstly, the shape
\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}
has a coefficient of
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}\approx\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{4}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2}
.
The first shape
\sigma\circ\tau_{1}\circ\sigma^{T}
has a coefficient of
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\tau_{1}\circ\sigma^{T}}\approx\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{4}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{4}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{4}
and with high probability, upto lower order terms,
\left\lVert M_{\tau_{1}}\right\rVert\leq md
. So, the inequality
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\tau_{1}\circ\sigma^{T}}^{2}\left\lVert M_{\tau_{1}}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}
rearranges to
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}}
. But this is precisely one of the assumptions on
m
. Moreover, this also confirms that we need this assumption on
m
in order for our strategy to go through.

The second shape
\sigma\circ\tau_{2}\circ\sigma^{T}
has a coefficient of
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\tau_{2}\circ\sigma^{T}}\approx\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{4}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{4}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{8}
and with high probability, upto lower order terms,
\left\lVert M_{\tau_{2}}\right\rVert\leq m^{2}d
. So, the inequality
\lambda_{\sigma\circ\tau_{2}\circ\sigma^{T}}^{2}\left\lVert M_{\tau_{2}}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}\lambda_{\sigma\circ\sigma^{T}}
rearranges to
m^{2}\leq\frac{k^{2}d}{\lambda^{4}}
. But this is obtained simply by multiplying our assumptions on
m
, namely
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
and
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}}
.

Moreover, consider a shape of the form
\sigma\circ\tau_{3}\circ\sigma^{T}
where
\tau_{3}
is similar to
\tau_{2}
except it has
t
(instead of
3
) different circle vertices that are common neighbors to the top 2 square vertices. Analyzing our required inequality, we get for our strategy to go through,
m
has to satisfy
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{d}{k^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{t+1}}
. By taking
t
arbitrarily large, we can see that the condition
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
is needed.

So, we get that for our analysis to go through, the assumptions
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}}
and
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
are necessary. We will prove that in fact, these are sufficient. To do this, we use a charging argument that exploits the special structure of the shapes
\alpha
that appear in our decomposition of
\Lambda
and their coefficients
\lambda_{\alpha}
, as we obtained in
Definition

4.3
. For details, see
Section

3
.

Chapter 6
Quantitative bounds

In this chapter, we will prove the main Sum of Squares lower bounds
Theorem

4.1
,
Theorem

3.3
and
Theorem

2.3
by building on the qualitative bounds from
Chapter

5
. The material in this chapter is adapted from
[
potechin2020machinery
]
, however several typos have been fixed and the technical exposition has been improved.

1
Planted slightly denser subgraph: Full verification

In this section, we will prove our main theorem on Planted slightly denser subgraph,
Theorem

4.1
.

See
4.1

We will apply the machinery. Here, we choose
\varepsilon
in the theorem, not to be confused with the
\varepsilon
in
Theorem

4.1
, to be an arbitrarily small constant.
We build on the qualitative bounds (and use the same notation) from
Section

2
.
The result will follow once we verify the main conditions and apply the machinery.

1
Middle shape bounds

Lemma 1.1
.

Suppose
k\leq n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
. For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
,

\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{n^{C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\tau)|}}

Proof.

This result follows by plugging in the value of
S(\tau)
. Using
k\leq n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}(2(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2n^{C_{p}\varepsilon}})-1)^{|E(\tau)|}\leq\frac{1}{n^{C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\tau)|}}

Corollary 1.2
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
, we have

c(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)\leq 1

Proof.

Since
\tau
is a proper middle shape, we have
w(I_{\tau})=0
and
w(S_{\tau})=w(U_{\tau})
. This implies

n^{\frac{w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau})}{2}}=\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}
.
Since
\tau
is proper, every vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}
or
i\in V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}
has
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
and hence,
|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|\leq 4|E(\tau)|
. Also,
q=n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}}
. We can set
C_{V}
sufficiently small so that, using
Lemma

1.1
,

\displaystyle c(\tau)

\displaystyle B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)

\displaystyle=100(3D_{V})^{|U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}|+2|E(\tau)|}2^{|V(\tau)\setminus(U_{\tau}\cup V_{\tau})|}\cdot(6D_{V}\sqrt[4]{2eq})^{|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|}\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}\cdot|E(\tau)|}\cdot\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}\cdot|E(\tau)|}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\tau)|}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

We can now obtain middle shape bounds.

Lemma 1.3
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
,

\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt=\begin{bmatrix}\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}&0\\
0&\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+B_{norm}(\tau)\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

By
Lemma

2.7
,
\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0
, so the second term above is positive semidefinite. For the first term, by
Lemma

2.5
,
H_{Id_{U}}\succeq 0
and by
Corollary

1.2
,
\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\geq 0
, which proves that the first term is also positive semidefinite.

2
Intersection term bounds

Lemma 1.4
.

Suppose
k\leq n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
. For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+|E(\gamma)|)}}

for some constant
B
that depends only on
C_{p}
. In particular, it is independent of
C_{V}
.

Proof.

Since
\gamma
is a left shape, we have
|U_{\gamma}|\geq|V_{\gamma}|
as
V_{\gamma}
is the unique minimum vertex separator of
\gamma
and so,
n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}=n^{|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|}\leq n^{|V(\gamma)|-\frac{|U_{\gamma}|+|V_{\gamma}|}{2}}
. Also, note that
2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|=|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|\geq|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|
. Therefore,

\displaystyle n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})|}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}(2(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2n^{C_{p}\varepsilon}})-1)^{2|E(\gamma)|}

\displaystyle\leq n^{|V(\gamma)|-\frac{|U_{\gamma}|+|V_{\gamma}|}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\right)^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2C_{p}\varepsilon}}\right)^{|E(\gamma)|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2C_{p}\varepsilon}}\right)^{|E(\gamma)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

for a constant
B
that depends only on
C_{p}
.

We obtain intersection term bounds.

Lemma 1.5
.

For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}\preceq H_{\gamma}^{\prime}

Proof.

By
Lemma

2.8
, we have

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle=c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}H^{\prime}_{\gamma}

Using the same proof as in
Lemma

2.5
, we can see that
H^{\prime}_{\gamma}\succeq 0
. Therefore, it suffices to prove that
c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}\leq 1
.
Since
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
,
|Aut(U)|=|U|!,|Aut(V)|=|V|!
. Therefore,
\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}=\frac{|U|!}{|V|!}\leq D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}
. Also,
q=n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}}
. Let
B
be the constant from
Lemma

1.4
. We can set
C_{V}
sufficiently small so that, using
Lemma

1.4
,

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}

\displaystyle\leq 100^{2}(3D_{V})^{2|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|+4|E(\alpha)|}4^{|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})|}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\cdot(3D_{V})^{4|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}(6D_{V}\sqrt[4]{2eq})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}\cdot D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

3
Truncation error bounds

In this section, we will prove truncation error bounds.
We use the strategy and notation from
[
potechin2020machinery
, Section 10]
.
First, we will need a bound on
B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
that is obtained below.

Lemma 1.6
.

Suppose
k\leq n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
. For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U}
,

B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon|V(\alpha)|+C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\alpha)|}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|U|}

Proof.

Let
\alpha=\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}
. Observe that
|V(\sigma)|+|V(\sigma^{\prime})|=|V(\alpha)|+|U|
. By choosing
C_{V}
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(6D_{V}\sqrt[4]{2eq})^{|V(\sigma)\setminus U_{\sigma}|+|V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma))-w(U)}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\cdot(6D_{V}\sqrt[4]{2eq})^{|V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|+|V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\sigma^{\prime}}|}n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma^{\prime}))-w(U)}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\cdot\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|V(\alpha)|}(2(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2n^{C_{p}\varepsilon}})-1)^{|E(\alpha)|}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{V}\cdot|V(\alpha)|}\sqrt{n}^{|V(\sigma)|-|U|}\sqrt{n}^{|V(\sigma^{\prime})|-|U|}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|V(\alpha)|}\frac{1}{n^{C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\alpha)|}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon|V(\alpha)|+C_{p}\varepsilon|E(\alpha)|}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|U|}

Now, we are ready to apply the strategy.

Lemma 1.7
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq\frac{1}{n^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}Id_{sym}

for a constant
K_{1}>0
.

Proof.

For
V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
, we have
\lambda_{V}=\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{|V|}
. Now, we choose
w_{V}=\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{D_{sos}-|V|}
. Then, for all
\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
, we have
w_{V}\leq\frac{w_{U_{\sigma}}\lambda_{U_{\sigma}}}{|\mathcal{I}_{mid}|B_{norm}(\sigma)^{2}{c(\sigma)^{2}}{H_{Id_{V}}(\sigma,\sigma)}}
which is easily verified using
Lemma

1.6
. The result now follows.

Lemma 1.8
.

\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}\leq\frac{n^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}

for a constant
K_{2}>0
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{U_{\gamma}}:|V(\sigma)|\leq D_{V},|V(\sigma^{\prime})|\leq D_{V},\atop|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}\text{ or }|V(\sigma^{\prime}\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}}{B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U_{\gamma}}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}

The set of
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}
that could appear in the above sum must necessarily be non-trivial and hence,
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U}^{\prime}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}{B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}:|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}\text{ or }|V(\sigma^{\prime}\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

For
\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{U}
, define
m_{\sigma}=D_{V}+1-|V(\sigma)|\geq 1
. This is precisely set so that for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}
, we have
|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}
if and only if
|V(\gamma)|\geq|U|+m_{\sigma}
. So, for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{U}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}:|V(\sigma\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}\text{ or }|V(\sigma^{\prime}\circ\gamma)|>D_{V}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}:|V(\gamma)|\geq|U|+\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

Also, for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U}^{\prime}
, we have
|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|+min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1\geq D_{V}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}

\displaystyle\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}{B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\frac{1}{2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{n^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{n^{0.5\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

where we used
Lemma

1.6
. Using
n^{0.5\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\geq n^{0.1\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{n^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{n^{0.1\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{n^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{n^{0.1\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{D_{V}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{n^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}n^{0.1\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{D_{V}}}

The final step will be to argue that
\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}n^{0.1\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}}\leq 1
which will complete the proof. But this will follow if we set
C_{V}
small enough.

We conclude the following.

Lemma 1.9
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

Proof.

Choose
C_{sos}
sufficiently small so that
\frac{1}{n^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}\geq 6\frac{n^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}
which can be satisfied by setting
C_{sos}<K_{3}C_{V}
for a sufficiently small constant
K_{3}>0
. Then, since
Id_{Sym}\succeq 0
, using
Lemma

1.7
and
Lemma

1.8
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{1}{n^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq 6\frac{n^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

2
Tensor PCA: Full verification

In this section, we will prove all the bounds required to prove
Theorem

3.3
.

See
3.3

We reuse the notation and qualitative bounds from
Section

3
.
Once we verify the conditions, this theorem will simply follow from the machinery.

1
Middle shape bounds

Lemma 2.1
.

Suppose
\lambda\leq n^{\frac{k}{4}-\varepsilon}
. For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
, suppose
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
, then

\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

Proof.

Firstly, we claim that
\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}kl_{e}\geq 2(|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|)
. For any vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
,
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even and is not
0
, hence,
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 2
. Any vertex
i\in U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
cannot have
deg^{\tau}(i)=0
otherwise
U_{\tau}\setminus\{i\}
is a vertex separator of strictly smaller weight than
U_{\tau}
, which is not possible, hence,
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}kl_{e}=\sum_{i\in V(\tau)}deg^{\tau}(i)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)+\sum_{i\in U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)+\sum_{i\in V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)

\displaystyle\geq 2|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}|

\displaystyle=2(|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|)

By choosing
C_{\Delta}
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\Delta^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}\left(\frac{\lambda}{(\Delta n)^{\frac{k}{2}}}\right)^{l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\Delta^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}n^{(-\frac{k}{4}-0.5\varepsilon)l_{e}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|-\frac{\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}kl_{e}}{2}}\Delta^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}n^{-0.5\varepsilon l_{e}}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}n^{-0.5\varepsilon l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

Corollary 2.2
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
, we have

c(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)\leq 1

Proof.

Since
\tau
is a proper middle shape, we have
w(I_{\tau})=0
and
w(S_{\tau,min})=w(U_{\tau})
. This implies

n^{\frac{w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau,min})}{2}}=\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}
.
If
deg^{\tau}(i)
is odd for any vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
, then
S(\tau)=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
. As was observed in the proof of
Lemma

2.1
, every vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}
or
i\in V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}
has
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
and hence,
|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|\leq 4\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}
. Also,
|E(\tau)|\leq\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}
and
q=n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})}
. We can set
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small so that, using
Lemma

2.1
,

\displaystyle c(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)

\displaystyle=100(3D_{V})^{|U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}|+k|E(\tau)|}2^{|V(\tau)\setminus(U_{\tau}\cup V_{\tau})|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot 2e(6qD_{V})^{|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{l_{e}}\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\cdot\sqrt{n}^{|V(\tau)|-|U_{\tau}|}S(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

We can now show middle shape bounds.

Lemma 2.3
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
,

\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}&0\\
0&\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+B_{norm}(\tau)\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

By
Lemma

3.8
,
\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0
, so the second term above is positive semidefinite. For the first term, by
Lemma

3.6
,
H_{Id_{U}}\succeq 0
and by
Corollary

2.2
,
\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\geq 0
, which proves that the first term is also positive semidefinite.

2
Intersection term bounds

Lemma 2.4
.

Suppose
\lambda\leq n^{\frac{k}{4}-\varepsilon}
. For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

for some constant
B
that depends only on
C_{\Delta}
. In particular, it is independent of
C_{V}
and
C_{E}
.

Proof.

Suppose there is a vertex
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
such that
deg^{\gamma}(i)
is odd, then
S(\gamma)=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume
deg^{\gamma}(i)
is even for all vertices
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
.
We first claim that
k\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}\geq 2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|
. Since
\gamma
is a left shape, all vertices
i
in
V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}
have
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 1
. In particular, all vertices
i\in V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}
have
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 1
.
Moreover, if
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
, since
deg^{\gamma}(i)
is even, we must have
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 2
.

Let
S^{\prime}
be the set of vertices
i\in U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
that have
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 1
. Then, note that
|S^{\prime}|+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|\geq|V_{\gamma}|\Longrightarrow|S^{\prime}|\geq|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|
since otherwise
S^{\prime}\cup(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
will be a vertex separator of
\gamma
of weight strictly less than
V_{\gamma}
, which is not possible. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}kl_{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in V(\gamma)}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq 2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|S^{\prime}|+|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|

\displaystyle\geq 2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|

\displaystyle=2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|

Finally, note that
2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|=|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|\geq|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|
. By choosing
C_{\Delta}
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})|}\Delta^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(\Delta n)^{k}}\right)^{l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})|}\Delta^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}n^{-(\frac{k}{2}+\varepsilon)l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{2|V(\gamma)|-|U_{\gamma}|-|V_{\gamma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}n^{-\varepsilon l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

for a constant
B
that depends only on
C_{\Delta}
.

Remark 2.5
.

In the above bounds, note that there is a decay of
n^{B\varepsilon}
for each vertex in
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
. One of the main technical reasons for introducing the slack parameter
C_{\Delta}
in the planted distribution was to introduce this decay, which is needed in the current machinery.

We can now obtain the intersection term bounds.

Lemma 2.6
.

For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}\preceq H_{\gamma}^{\prime}

Proof.

By
Lemma

3.9
, we have

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle\preceq c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}H^{\prime}_{\gamma}

Using the same proof as in
Lemma

3.6
, we can see that
H^{\prime}_{\gamma}\succeq 0
. Therefore, it suffices to prove that
c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}\leq 1
.
Since
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
,
|Aut(U)|=|U|!,|Aut(V)|=|V|!
. Therefore,
\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}=\frac{|U|!}{|V|!}\leq D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}
. Also,
|E(\gamma)|\leq\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}
and
q=n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})}
. Let
B
be the constant from
Lemma

2.4
. We can set
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small so that, using
Lemma

2.4
,

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}

\displaystyle\leq 100^{2}(3D_{V})^{2|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2k|E(\alpha)|}4^{|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot(3D_{V})^{4|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}(6qD_{V})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{2l_{e}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}\cdot D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

3
Truncation error bounds

In this section, we will obtain the truncation error bounds using the strategy sketched in section 10 of
[
potechin2020machinery
]
. We also reuse the notation. First, we need the following bound on
B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Suppose
\lambda=n^{\frac{k}{4}-\varepsilon}
. For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U}
,

B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}n^{|U|}}

Proof.

Suppose there is a vertex
i\in V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}
such that
deg^{\sigma}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma}}(i)
is odd, then
H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume that
deg^{\sigma}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma}}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}
. Similarly, assume that
deg^{\sigma^{\prime}}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\sigma^{\prime}}
. Also, if
\rho_{\sigma}\neq\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, we will have
H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=0
and we’d be done. So, assume
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Let
\alpha=\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}
. We will first prove that
\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}kl_{e}+2deg(\alpha)\geq 2|V(\alpha)|+2|U|
. Firstly, note that all vertices
i\in V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})
have
deg^{\alpha}(i)
to be even and nonzero, and hence at least
2
. Moreover, in both the sets
U_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})
and
V_{\alpha}\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha})
, there are at least
|U|-|U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}|
vertices of degree at least
1
, because
U
is a minimum vertex separator. Also, note that
deg(\alpha)\geq|U_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}|
. This implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}kl_{e}

\displaystyle+2deg(\alpha)

\displaystyle\geq 2|V(\alpha)\setminus(U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha})|+2(|U|-|U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}|)+2(|U_{\alpha}|+|V_{\alpha}|)

\displaystyle=2(|V(\alpha)|-|U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha}|)+2(|U|-|U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}|)+2(|U_{\alpha}\cup V_{\alpha}|+|U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}|)

\displaystyle=2|V(\alpha)|+2|U|

where we used the fact that
U_{\alpha}\cap V_{\alpha}\subseteq U
. Finally, by choosing
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2e(6qD_{V})^{|V(\sigma)\setminus U_{\sigma}|+|V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\sigma)}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{l_{e}}n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma))-w(U)}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot 2e(6qD_{V})^{|V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|+|V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\sigma^{\prime}}|}\prod_{e\in E(\sigma^{\prime})}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{l_{e}}n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma^{\prime}))-w(U)}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\Delta^{|V(\alpha)|}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta n}}\right)^{deg(\alpha)}\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}\left(\frac{\lambda}{(\Delta n)^{\frac{k}{2}}}\right)^{l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\alpha)|+\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e})}\Delta^{|V(\alpha)|}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\right)^{deg(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sqrt{n}^{|V(\alpha)|-|U|}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{deg(\alpha)}\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}n^{(-\frac{k}{4}-0.5\varepsilon)l_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\alpha)|+\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e})}}{n^{\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\alpha)|}n^{0.5\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\Delta^{D_{sos}}n^{|U|}}\sqrt{n}^{|V(\alpha)|+|U|-deg(\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}kl_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\alpha)|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}n^{|U|}}

where we used the facts
\Delta\leq 1,deg(\alpha)\leq 2D_{sos}
.

We now apply the strategy by showing the following bounds.

Lemma 2.8
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq\frac{\Delta^{2D_{sos}^{2}}}{n^{D_{sos}}}Id_{sym}

Proof.

For
V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
,
\lambda_{V}=\frac{1}{n^{|V|}}
. We then choose
w_{V}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{D_{sos}-|V|}
. For all left shapes
\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
, it’s easy to verify
w_{V}\leq\frac{w_{U_{\sigma}}\lambda_{U_{\sigma}}}{|\mathcal{I}_{mid}|B_{norm}(\sigma)^{2}{c(\sigma)^{2}}{H_{Id_{V}}(\sigma,\sigma)}}
using
Lemma

2.7
. This completes the proof.

Lemma 2.9
.

\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}\leq\frac{1}{\Delta^{2D_{sos}}2^{D_{V}}}

Proof.

We use the same argument and notation as in
Lemma

1.8
. When we plug in the bounds, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}

\displaystyle\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}{B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\frac{1}{2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}n^{|U|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{n^{0.5\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

where we used
Lemma

2.7
. Using
n^{0.5C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\geq n^{0.1\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}

\displaystyle\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{n^{0.1\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{n^{0.1\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{D_{sos}}2^{D_{V}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}n^{0.1\varepsilon C_{\Delta}|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\Delta^{2D_{sos}}2^{D_{V}}}

where we set
C_{sos}
small enough so that
D_{sos}=n^{\varepsilon C_{sos}}\leq n^{c\varepsilon C_{\Delta}}=\frac{1}{\Delta}
. The final step will be to argue that
\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}n^{0.1C_{\Delta}\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}}\leq 1
which will complete the proof. But this will follow if we set
C_{V},C_{E}
small enough.

We can finally complete the analysis of the truncation error.

Lemma 2.10
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

Proof.

Choose
C_{sos}
sufficiently small so that
\frac{\Delta^{2D_{sos}^{2}}}{n^{D_{sos}}}\geq\frac{6}{\Delta^{2D_{sos}}2^{D_{V}}}
which is satisfied by setting
C_{sos}<0.5C_{V}
. Then, since
Id_{Sym}\succeq 0
, using
Lemma

2.8
and
Lemma

2.9
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{\Delta^{2D_{sos}^{2}}}{n^{D_{sos}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{6}{\Delta^{2D_{sos}}2^{D_{V}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

3
Sparse PCA: Full verification

In this section, we will full prove
Theorem

2.3
.

See
2.3

We already showed the relevant qualitative bounds in
Section

4
. We use the bounds and also the notation from that section.
We will apply the machinery.

Definition 3.1
.

Define
n=\max(d,m)
.

The above definition conforms with the notation used in the machinery. So, we can use the bounds as stated there.
Once we verify the conditions, the theorem will immediately follow from the machinery.

1
Middle shape bounds

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose
0<A<\frac{1}{4}
is a constant such that
\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon}
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-2A}
. For all
m
such that
m\leq\frac{d^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}},m\leq\frac{k^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
, for all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
, suppose
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
, then

\sqrt{d}^{|\tau|_{1}-|U_{\tau}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|\tau|_{2}-|U_{\tau}|_{2}}S(\tau)\leq\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}}(deg^{\tau}(j)-1)!!\cdot\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

Proof.

Let
r_{1}=|\tau|_{1}-|U_{\tau}|_{1},r_{2}=|\tau|_{2}-|U_{\tau}|_{2}
. Since
\Delta\leq 1
, it suffices to prove

E:=\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

We will need the following claim.

Claim 3.3
.

\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}\geq 2\max(r_{1},r_{2})
.

Proof.

We will first prove
\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}\geq 2r_{1}
. For any vertex
i\in V_{1}(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
,
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even and is not
0
, hence,
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 2
. Any vertex
i\in U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
cannot have
deg^{\tau}(i)=0
otherwise
U_{\tau}\setminus\{i\}
is a vertex separator of strictly smaller weight than
U_{\tau}
, which is not possible, hence,
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
. Similarly, for
i\in V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}
,
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
. Also, since
H_{\tau}
is bipartite, we have
\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\tau)}deg^{\tau}(i)=\sum_{j\in V_{2}(\tau)}deg^{\tau}(j)=\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}
. Consider

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\tau)}deg^{\tau}(i)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)+\sum_{i\in(U_{\tau})_{1}\setminus V_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)+\sum_{i\in(V_{\tau})_{1}\setminus U_{\tau}}deg^{\tau}(i)

\displaystyle\geq 2|V_{1}(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|(U_{\tau})_{1}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|(V_{\tau})_{1}\setminus U_{\tau}|

\displaystyle=2r_{1}

We can similarly prove
\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}\geq 2r_{2}

To illustrate the main idea, we will start by proving the weaker bound
E\leq 1
. Observe that our assumptions imply
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}},m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
and also, using the fact
\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon}\leq 1
, we have
E\leq\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2\max(r_{1},r_{2})}
.

Claim 3.4
.

For integers
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0
, if
m\leq\frac{d}{\lambda^{2}}
and
m\leq\frac{k^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}
, then,

\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2\max(r_{1},r_{2})}\leq 1

Proof.

We will consider the cases
r_{1}\geq r_{2}
and
r_{1}<r_{2}
separately. If
r_{1}\geq r_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

And if
r_{1}<r_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}-r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{2}-r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{2}}

\displaystyle=1

For the desired bounds, we mimic this argument while carefully keeping track of factors of
d^{\varepsilon}
.

Claim 3.5
.

For integers
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0
and an integer
r\geq 2\max(r_{1},r_{2})
, if
m\leq\frac{d^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
and
m\leq\frac{k^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
, then,

\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r}\leq\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{r}

Proof.

If
r_{1}\geq r_{2}
,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}^{1-\varepsilon}}\right)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{r-2r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{d^{2A}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{r-2r_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{r}

And if
r_{1}<r_{2}
,

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{2}-r_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{2}-r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{r_{1}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{2r_{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{r-2r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{d^{2A}}\right)^{\varepsilon r_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{r-2r_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon}}\right)^{\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}

The result follows by setting
r=\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}
in the above claim.

Corollary 3.6
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
, we have

c(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)R(\tau)\leq 1

Proof.

First, note that if
deg^{\tau}(i)
is odd for any vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
, then
S(\tau)=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume that
deg^{\tau}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}
.
Since
\tau
is a proper middle shape, we have
w(I_{\tau})=0
and
w(S_{\tau,min})=w(U_{\tau})
. This implies

n^{\frac{w(V(\tau))+w(I_{\tau})-w(S_{\tau,min})}{2}}=\sqrt{d}^{|\tau|_{1}-|U_{\tau}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|\tau|_{2}-|U_{\tau}|_{2}}
.
As was observed in the proof of
Lemma

3.2
, every vertex
i\in V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}
or
i\in V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}
has
deg^{\tau}(i)\geq 1
and hence,
|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|\leq 4\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}
. Also,
q=d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})}
. We can set
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle c(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)S(\tau)R(\tau)

\displaystyle=100(6D_{V})^{|U_{\tau}\setminus V_{\tau}|+|V_{\tau}\setminus U_{\tau}|+2|E(\tau)|}4^{|V(\tau)\setminus(U_{\tau}\cup V_{\tau})|}

\displaystyle\cdot 2e(6qD_{V})^{|V(\tau)\setminus U_{\tau}|+|V(\tau)\setminus V_{\tau}|}\prod_{e\in E(\tau)}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{l_{e}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sqrt{d}^{|\tau|_{1}-|U_{\tau}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|\tau|_{2}-|U_{\tau}|_{2}}S(\tau)(C_{disc}\sqrt{D_{E}})^{\sum_{j\in(U_{\tau})_{2}\cup(V_{\tau})_{2}}deg^{\tau}(j)}

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)\cdot(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\cdot\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\tau)\setminus V_{2}(U_{\tau})\setminus V_{2}(V_{\tau})}(deg^{\tau}(j)-1)!!\cdot\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)\cdot(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\cdot(D_{V}D_{E})^{\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

We can now obtain our desired middle shape bounds.

Lemma 3.7
.

For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\tau\in{\mathcal{M}}_{U}
,

\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}&B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}\\
B_{norm}(\tau)H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}&0\\
0&\left(\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\right)H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+B_{norm}(\tau)\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}

By
Lemma

4.9
,
\begin{bmatrix}\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}&H_{\tau}\\
H_{\tau}^{T}&\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}H_{Id_{U}}\end{bmatrix}\succeq 0
, so the second term above is positive semidefinite. For the first term, by
Lemma

4.5
,
H_{Id_{U}}\succeq 0
and by
Corollary

3.6
,
\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|c(\tau)}-\frac{S(\tau)R(\tau)B_{norm}(\tau)}{|Aut(U)|}\geq 0
, which proves that the first term is also positive semidefinite.

2
Intersection term bounds

Lemma 3.8
.

Suppose
0<A<\frac{1}{4}
is a constant such that
\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon},\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-2A}
and
\frac{k}{d}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon}
. For all
m
such that
m\leq\frac{d^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}},m\leq\frac{k^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
, for all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}\leq\left(\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}(deg^{\gamma}(j)-1)!!\right)^{2}\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

for some constant
B>0
that depends only on
C_{\Delta}
. In particular, it is independent of
C_{V}
and
C_{E}
.

Proof.

Suppose there is a vertex
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
such that
deg^{\gamma}(i)
is odd, then
S(\gamma)=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume
deg^{\gamma}(i)
is even for all vertices
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
.
We have
n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}=d^{|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}}m^{|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}}
. Plugging in
S(\gamma)
, we get that we have to prove

\displaystyle E:=d^{|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}}m^{|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\Delta^{2|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}-|V_{\gamma}|_{2}}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\frac{\lambda^{l_{e}}}{k^{l_{e}}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

Let
S^{\prime}
be the set of vertices
i\in U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
that have
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 1
. Let
e,f
be the number of type
1
vertices and the number of type
2
vertices in
S^{\prime}
respectively. Observe that
S^{\prime}\cup(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
is a vertex separator of
\gamma
.
Let
g=|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|_{1}
(resp.
h=|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|_{2}
) be the number of type
1
vertices (resp. type
2
vertices) in
V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}
.
We first claim that
d^{e}m^{f}\geq d^{g}m^{h}
. To see this, note that the vertex separator
S^{\prime}\cup(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
has weight
\sqrt{d}^{e+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{f+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{2}}
. On the other hand,
V_{\gamma}
has weight
\sqrt{d}^{g+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{h+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{2}}
. Since
\gamma
is a left shape,
V_{\gamma}
is the unique minimum vertex separator and hence, we have the inequality
\sqrt{d}^{e+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{f+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{2}}\geq\sqrt{d}^{g+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{h+|U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma}|_{2}}
which implies
d^{e}m^{f}\geq d^{g}m^{h}
.
Let
p=|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})|_{1}
(resp.
q=|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})|_{2}
) be the number of type
1
vertices (resp. type
2
vertices) in
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})
.
To illustrate the main idea, we will first prove the weaker inequality
E\leq 1
. Since
\Delta\leq 1
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle d^{|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}}m^{|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\frac{\lambda^{l_{e}}}{k^{l_{e}}}\leq 1

We have

d^{|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}}m^{|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}}=d^{p+g}m^{q+h}\leq n^{p+\frac{e+g}{2}}m^{q+\frac{f+h}{2}}

since
d^{e}m^{f}\geq d^{g}m^{h}
. Also,
2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}=2p+e+g
. So, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle n^{p+\frac{e+g}{2}}m^{q+\frac{f+h}{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2p+e+g}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\left(\frac{\lambda}{k}\right)^{l_{e}}\leq 1

We will need the following claim.

Claim 3.9
.

\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}\geq\max(2p+e+g,2q+f+h)

Proof.

Since
H_{\gamma}
is bipartite, we have
\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}=\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\gamma)}deg^{\gamma}(i)=\sum_{i\in V_{2}(\gamma)}deg^{\gamma}(i)
. Observe that all vertices
i\in V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}
have
deg^{\gamma}(i)
nonzero and even, and hence,
deg^{\gamma}(i)\geq 2
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\gamma)}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in(U_{\gamma})_{1}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in(V_{\gamma})_{1}\setminus U_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq 2p+e+g

Similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in V_{2}(\gamma)}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in V_{2}(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in(U_{\gamma})_{2}\setminus V_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)+\sum_{i\in(V_{\gamma})_{2}\setminus U_{\gamma}}deg^{\gamma}(i)

\displaystyle\geq 2q+f+h

Therefore,
\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}\geq\max(2p+e+g,2q+f+h)
.

Now, let
r_{1}=p+\frac{e+g}{2},r_{2}=q+\frac{f+h}{2}
. Then,
\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}\geq 2\max(r_{1},r_{2})
and we wish to prove

d^{r_{1}}m^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2r_{1}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{k}\right)^{2\max(r_{1},r_{2})}\leq 1

This expression simply follows by squaring
3.4
.

Now, to prove that
E\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}
, we mimic this argument while carefully keeping track of factors of
d^{\varepsilon}
. Again, using
d^{e}m^{f}\geq d^{g}m^{h}
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle d^{p+\frac{e+g}{2}}m^{q+\frac{f+h}{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}}

\displaystyle\Delta^{2|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}-|V_{\gamma}|_{2}}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}\frac{\lambda^{l_{e}}}{k^{l_{e}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

The idea is that the
d^{B\varepsilon}
decay for the edges are obtained from the stronger assumption on
m
, namely
m\leq\frac{d^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}},m\leq\frac{k^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
. And the
d^{B\varepsilon}
decay for the type
1
vertices of
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
are obtained both from the stronger assumption on
m
as well as the factors of
\frac{k}{d}
, the latter especially useful for the degree
0
vertices. Finally, the
d^{B\varepsilon}
decay for the type
2
vertices of
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
are obtained from the factors of
\Delta
.
Indeed, note that for a constant
B
that depends on
C_{\Delta}
,
\Delta^{2|\gamma|_{2}-|U_{\gamma}|_{2}-|V_{\gamma}|_{2}}\leq d^{-B\varepsilon|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|_{2}}
. So, we would be done if we prove

\displaystyle d^{p+\frac{e+g}{2}}m^{q+\frac{f+h}{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{k}\right)^{\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|_{1}+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

Let
c_{0}
be the number of type
1
vertices
i
in
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
such that
deg^{\gamma}(i)=0
. Since they have degree
0
, they must be in
(U_{\gamma})_{1}\setminus V_{\gamma}
. Also, we have
2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}=2p+e+g+c_{0}
and hence,
\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2|\gamma|_{1}-|U_{\gamma}|_{1}-|V_{\gamma}|_{1}}=\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2p+e+g+c_{0}}
. For these degree
0
vertices, we have that the factors of
\frac{k}{d}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon}
offer a decay of
\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon}}
. Therefore, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle d^{p+\frac{e+g}{2}}m^{q+\frac{f+h}{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2p+e+g}\left(\frac{\lambda}{k}\right)^{\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(p+q+e+f+g+h)+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

for a constant
B>0
. Observe that
p+q+e+f+g+h\leq 2(\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})
. Therefore, using the notation
r_{1}=p+\frac{e+g}{2},r_{2}=q+\frac{f+h}{2}
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle d^{r_{1}}m^{r_{2}}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{2r_{1}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{k}\right)^{\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}}

for a constant
B>0
. But this follows by squaring
3.5
where we set
r=\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}
.

Remark 3.10
.

In the above bounds, note that there is a decay of
d^{B\varepsilon}
for each vertex in
V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})
. One of the main technical reasons for introducing the slack parameter
C_{\Delta}
in the planted distribution was to introduce this decay, which is needed in the current machinery.

With this, we obtain intersection term bounds.

Lemma 3.11
.

For all
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
where
w(U)>w(V)
and all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,V}
,

c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}\preceq H_{\gamma}^{\prime}

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.10
, we have

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}H_{Id_{V}}^{-\gamma,\gamma}

\displaystyle\preceq c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}H^{\prime}_{\gamma}

Using the same proof as in
Lemma

4.5
, we can see that
H^{\prime}_{\gamma}\succeq 0
. Therefore, it suffices to prove that
c(\gamma)^{2}N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}\leq 1
.
Since
U,V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
,
Aut(U)=|U|_{1}!|U|_{2}!,Aut(V)=|V|_{1}!|V|_{2}!
. Therefore,
\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}=\frac{|U|_{1}!|U|_{2}!}{|V|_{1}!|V|_{2}!}\leq D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}
. Also,
|E(\gamma)|\leq\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}
and
q=d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})}
. Note
R(\gamma)^{2}=(C_{disc}\sqrt{D_{E}})^{2\sum_{j\in(U_{\gamma})_{2}\cup(V_{\gamma})_{2}}deg^{\gamma}(j)}\leq d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{E}\cdot\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}
and

\left(\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}}(deg^{\gamma}(j)-1)!!\right)^{2}\leq(D_{V}D_{E})^{2\sum_{e\in E(\tau)}l_{e}}\leq d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}

Let
B
be the constant from
Lemma

3.8
. We can set
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small so that, using
Lemma

3.8
,

\displaystyle c(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle N(\gamma)^{2}B(\gamma)^{2}S(\gamma)^{2}R(\gamma)^{2}\frac{|Aut(U)|}{|Aut(V)|}

\displaystyle\leq 100^{2}(6D_{V})^{2|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V_{\gamma}\setminus U_{\gamma}|+|E(\alpha)|}16^{|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cup V_{\gamma})|}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot(3D_{V})^{4|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|}(6qD_{V})^{2|V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma}|+2|V(\gamma)\setminus V_{\gamma}|}\prod_{e\in E(\gamma)}(400D_{V}^{2}D_{E}^{2}q)^{2l_{e}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon C_{E}\cdot\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e}}\cdot D_{V}^{|U_{\gamma}\setminus V_{\gamma}|}

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot n^{w(V(\gamma)\setminus U_{\gamma})}S(\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)\cdot\varepsilon(C_{V}+C_{E})\cdot(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{B\varepsilon(|V(\gamma)\setminus(U_{\gamma}\cap V_{\gamma})|+\sum_{e\in E(\gamma)}l_{e})}}

\displaystyle\leq 1

3
Truncation error bounds

In this section, we will obtain truncation error bounds using the strategy sketched in
[
potechin2020machinery
, Section 10]
. We also reuse the notation. To do this, we need to first obtain a bound on the quantity
B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Suppose
0<A<\frac{1}{4}
is a constant such that
\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-A\varepsilon}
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq d^{-2A}
. Suppose
m
is such that
m\leq\frac{d^{1-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}},m\leq\frac{k^{2-\varepsilon}}{\lambda^{2}}
. For all
U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{U}
,

B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{d^{0.5A\varepsilon(|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|+\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)l_{e}}}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{|U_{\sigma}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}}m^{|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}}}

Proof.

Suppose there is a vertex
i\in V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}
such that
deg^{\sigma}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma}}(i)
is odd, then
H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=0
and the inequality is true. So, assume that
deg^{\sigma}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma}}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\sigma)\setminus V_{\sigma}
. Similarly, assume that
deg^{\sigma^{\prime}}(i)+deg^{U_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(i)
is even for all
i\in V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus V_{\sigma^{\prime}}
. Also, if
\rho_{\sigma}\neq\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, we will have
H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=0
and we would be done. So, assume
\rho_{\sigma}=\rho_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Let there be
e
(resp.
f
) vertices of type
1
(resp. type
2
) in
V(\sigma)\setminus U_{\sigma}\setminus V_{\sigma}
. Then,
n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma))-w(U)}{2}}=\sqrt{d}^{|V(\sigma)|_{1}-|U|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|V(\sigma)|_{2}-|U|_{2}}=\sqrt{d}^{|U_{\sigma}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|U_{\sigma}|_{2}}\sqrt{d}^{e}\sqrt{m}^{f}
. Let there be
g
(resp.
h
) vertices of type
1
(resp. type
2
) in
V(\sigma^{\prime})\setminus U_{\sigma^{\prime}}\setminus V_{\sigma^{\prime}}
. Then, similarly,
n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma^{\prime}))-w(U)}{2}}\leq\sqrt{d}^{|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}}\sqrt{m}^{|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}}\sqrt{d}^{g}\sqrt{m}^{h}
.

Let
\alpha=\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}
. Since all vertices in
V(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}
have degree at least
2
, we have
\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}\geq\sum_{i\in V_{1}(\alpha)\setminus U_{\alpha}\setminus V_{\alpha}}deg^{\alpha}(i)\geq 2(e+g)+|U_{\sigma}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma}|_{2}
. Similarly,
\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}\geq 2(f+h)+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}
. Therefore, by setting
r_{1}=e+g,r_{2}=f+h
in
3.5
, we have

\sqrt{d}^{e+g}\sqrt{m}^{f+h}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{e+g}\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}^{l_{e}}}{\sqrt{k}^{l_{e}}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}}

Also,

\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{|\alpha|_{1}}\leq\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{e+g+|U_{\sigma}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}}

and

\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\alpha)}(deg^{\alpha}(j)-1)!!\leq d^{\varepsilon C_{V}\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}

Therefore,

\displaystyle n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma))-w(U)}{2}}n^{\frac{w(V(\sigma^{\prime}))-w(U)}{2}}H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)D_{sos}}\sqrt{d}^{e}\sqrt{m}^{f}d^{O(1)D_{sos}}\sqrt{d}^{g}\sqrt{m}^{h}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\frac{1}{|Aut(U)|}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)^{deg(\alpha)}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{|\alpha|_{1}}\Delta^{|\alpha|_{2}}\prod_{j\in V_{2}(\alpha)}(deg^{\alpha}(j)-1)!!\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}^{l_{e}}}{\sqrt{k}^{l_{e}}}

\displaystyle\leq d^{O(1)D_{sos}}d^{\varepsilon C_{V}\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}\sqrt{d}^{e+g}\sqrt{m}^{f+h}\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)^{e+g}\prod_{e\in E(\alpha)}\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}^{l_{e}}}{\sqrt{k}^{l_{e}}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{|U_{\sigma}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}}m^{|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d^{\varepsilon C_{V}\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}}{d^{A\varepsilon\sum_{e\in E(\alpha)}l_{e}}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{|U_{\sigma}|_{1}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{1}}m^{|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}+|U_{\sigma^{\prime}}|_{2}}}

By setting
C_{V},C_{E}
sufficiently small and plugging in the expressions for
B_{norm}(\sigma),B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})
, we obtain the result.

We can apply the the strategy now.

Lemma 3.13
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq\frac{1}{d^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}Id_{sym}

for a constant
K_{1}>0
that can depend on
C_{\Delta}
.

Proof.

For
V\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}
,
\lambda_{V}=\frac{\Delta^{|V|_{2}}}{d^{|V|_{1}}k^{|V|_{2}}}
. Let the minimum value of this quantity over all
V
be
N
. We then choose
w_{V}=N/\lambda_{V}
so that for all left shapes
\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}_{V}
,
Lemma

3.12
implies
w_{V}\leq\frac{w_{U_{\sigma}}\lambda_{U_{\sigma}}}{|\mathcal{I}_{mid}|B_{norm}(\sigma)^{2}{c(\sigma)^{2}}{H_{Id_{V}}(\sigma,\sigma)}}
, completing the proof.

Lemma 3.14
.

\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}\leq\frac{d^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}

for a constant
K_{2}>0
that can depend on
C_{\Delta}
.

Proof.

We do the same calculations as in the proof of
Lemma

1.8
, until

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}

\displaystyle\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}{B_{norm}(\sigma)B_{norm}(\sigma^{\prime})H_{Id_{U}}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\frac{1}{2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{d^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{d^{0.5A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

where we used
Lemma

3.12
. Using
d^{0.5A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}\geq d^{0.1A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H_{Id_{U}},H^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{d^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{d^{0.1A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{\min(m_{\sigma},m_{\sigma^{\prime}})-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{d^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{d^{0.1A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{D_{V}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{d^{O(1)D_{sos}}}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}d^{0.1A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}2^{D_{V}}}

The final step will be to argue that
\sum_{U\in{\mathcal{I}}_{mid}}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{U}}\frac{1}{D_{sos}^{D_{sos}}d^{0.1A\varepsilon|V(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime})|}}\leq 1
which will complete the proof. But this will follow if we set
C_{V},C_{E}
small enough.

We can finally show that truncation errors can be handled.

Lemma 3.15
.

Whenever
\left\lVert M_{\alpha}\right\rVert\leq B_{norm}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,

\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

Proof.

Choose
C_{sos}
sufficiently small so that
\frac{1}{d^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}\geq 6\frac{d^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}
which can be satisfied by setting
C_{sos}<K_{3}C_{V}
for a sufficiently small constant
K_{3}>0
. Then, since
Id_{Sym}\succeq 0
, using
Lemma

3.13
and
Lemma

3.14
,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{M^{fact}_{Id_{U}}{(H_{Id_{U}})}}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{1}{d^{K_{1}D_{sos}^{2}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq 6\frac{d^{K_{2}D_{sos}}}{2^{D_{V}}}Id_{sym}

\displaystyle\succeq 6\left(\sum_{U\in\mathcal{I}_{mid}}{\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{U,*}}{\frac{d_{Id_{U}}(H^{\prime}_{\gamma},H_{Id_{U}})}{|Aut(U)|c(\gamma)}}}\right)Id_{sym}

Chapter 7
Followup and Future work

In this chapter, we go over some follow-up works that are not covered in this dissertation and also suggest directions for future work. We then conclude this dissertation with a note on the broader implications of our work for computer science.

1
Nonlinear concentration for non-product distributions

Our techniques in
Chapter

1
apply to a collection of random variables that are sampled independently of each other. A natural question is to ask if we can generalize to the case when they are not independent. For example, this is useful when instead of analyzing Erdős-Rényi random graphs, we wish to analyze uniform
d
-regular graphs. Such a generalization seems extremely likely because our proof techniques essentially requires a Markov Chain that mixes rapidly to the given distribution, and then we can recursively apply the Poincaré inequality. We leave this for future work.

2
Sum of Squares lower bounds

In this dissertation, we saw several SoS lower bounds and while they build on fundamental conceptual building blocks such as the nonlinear concentration results we show and simple heuristics like pseudocalibration, an important technical barrier in the current proofs is that the proofs are highly technical and have many moving parts. It’s an important research question to understand if the proofs can be simplified. Apart from enabling a better understanding of the SoS hierarchy, this will also help us understand the computational barriers of several fundamental problems in computer science. Examples of such problems follow.

1
Sparse Independent Set

In a follow-up work
[
jones2022sum
]
, we prove SoS lower bounds for the important problem of maximum independent set on sparse Erdős-Rényi random graphs.

In this dissertation, the SoS lower bounds studied were in the setting when the input was sampled from product distributions where each distribution was either Rademacher or Gaussian. This is also the case in many prior works on SoS lower bounds. Recall that this was termed the
dense setting
in
Chapter

1
. It’s equally important to study problems in the fascinating average-case
sparse setting
where the input distribution could have high Orlicz norm, for example when the input is an Erdős-Rényi random graph sampled from
G_{n,p}
instead of
G_{n,\frac{1}{2}}
for some
p=o(1)
. The techniques developed in this work and prior works for high degree SoS lower bounds do not easily generalize to this setting. The work
[
jones2022sum
]
initiates this research direction for the fundamental problem of maximum independent set on random sparse graphs.

Consider the independent set problem on a graph
G\sim G_{n,\frac{d}{n}}
where
d
is the average degree. If
d=\frac{n}{2}
, then this is the same as the maximum clique problem and SoS lower bounds were obtained in
[
BHKKMP16
]
. We now focus on the setting
d\ll n
. We first state the size of the true optimum.

Fact 2.1

(
[
COE15
,
DM11
,
DSS16
]
)
.

W.h.p. the max independent set in
G
has size
(1+o_{d}(1))\cdot\frac{2\ln d}{d}\cdot n
.

The famous Lovász
\vartheta
function efficiently computes an upper bound on this value and its value is well-known on such random graphs.

Fact 2.2

(
[
CO05
]
)
.

W.h.p.
\vartheta(G)=\Theta(\frac{n}{\sqrt{d}})
.

The value of the
\vartheta
function is also the output of the degree
2
